My husband had prostate surgery 8 weeks ago.
He'd been managing his bladder issues for 9 years. Quarterly urology visits. A prescription that was adjusted three times. A diet he followed without complaint. A PSA test every six months.
He left for his pre-op appointment at 7:30 on a Tuesday morning in the same flannel shirt he'd worn to every doctor's visit since he retired.
He came home with a catheter bag strapped to his leg and a face I didn't recognize.
He was 64 years old.
He took his tamsulosin every night at 9 PM. He cut the caffeine. He gave up the evening beers on the back porch that we'd shared every Friday night for 27 years because the urologist said alcohol irritated the bladder. He did the pelvic floor exercises from the printout they gave him. He tracked every bathroom trip in a small spiral notebook he kept in his back pocket.
And he was coded with an enlarged prostate and mildly elevated PSA on a routine exam dated April 14, 2016.
No doctor explained to us what was actually causing the prostate to swell until it was too late to do anything but cut.
I'm writing this from the recliner in our living room. Gary is asleep on the couch across from me with a pillow between his knees and the catheter bag on the floor next to a plastic basin the hospital sent home with us. The TV is on but neither of us is watching it. The back porch light is on because I forgot to turn it off and I don't want to walk past him to get to the switch because every time I move through the room he startles awake and grabs for the bag.
He had a TURP. Transurethral resection of the prostate. They put a scope through the tip of his penis and cut away the tissue that had been squeezing his urethra shut for the past 3 years. He was under general anesthesia for 90 minutes. When he woke up he had a catheter and blood in his urine and a nurse explaining that the burning sensation was normal and would last 4 to 6 weeks.
4 to 6 weeks.
He hasn't slept more than 2 hours at a stretch since the surgery. The burning wakes him. The urgency wakes him. The bag needs emptying. He stands in the bathroom doorway at 3 AM holding the doorframe with both hands and breathing through his teeth.
Before the surgery he was getting up 6 times a night. After the surgery — which was supposed to fix it — he's getting up 4. The urologist said that's progress. Gary looked at me in the parking lot after that appointment and didn't say a word. He just got in the truck and put both hands on the steering wheel and stared through the windshield.
I need to tell you what happened to Gary. Because every time I open Facebook I see another post about another man in his sixties whose prostate problems came out of nowhere and the comments are always the same.
That's just what happens when you get older. My doctor said my prostate was fine. Flomax works great for me. If it gets bad enough they can always do the surgery.
That's what we thought too. Until I sat on the floor of our bedroom closet 2 weeks after the surgery with Gary's medication bag in my lap and a printout from 2016 that nobody had ever explained to us.
So before I tell you the rest of this I want to tell you the part that matters.
Gary didn't ignore the warning signs.
His doctors managed the symptoms and never once told him what was actually making his prostate grow.
If you've ever had your PSA tested or your husband has or your father or your brother please read this entire thing.
I know it's long. I know your thumb is hovering over the screen. I know there are a hundred other things you could be doing.
8 weeks ago I would have traded the porch, the truck, the house, all of it, for someone to tell me what I'm about to tell you.
Gary has been my husband for 34 years.
He worked 31 of those years as an electrician out of IBEW Local 429 and in all 31 years he never once filed a disability claim. He kept his tool belt on the hook by the garage door and his lunch cooler on the counter and his work boots broken in exactly the way

4 to 6 weeks.

My Wife Stopped Trying To Have S*x With Me (And I Thought It Was Over).
For anyone who's noticed their wife pulling away, going cold, or just... giving up, here's what I didn't see coming.
I'm 47.
Married 18 years.
And for the last three of them, my wife stopped initiating sex.
Stopped wearing the lingerie I used to love.
Stopped trying, period.
I thought she'd lost interest.
That she didn't find me attractive anymore.
That maybe she'd checked out of our marriage emotionally.
But the truth was so much worse than that.
It turns out, she thought I'd given up on her.
See, I'd been having trouble getting hard.
Couldn't stay hard when we did try.
And like most guys, I went looking for answers online.
Every article said the same thing: porn.
Too much porn.
Fried dopamine.
Rewired brain.
You did this to yourself.
So I believed it.
I quit cold turkey.
Ninety days without porn or masturbation.
It was hell.
The urges.
The frustration.
But I was convinced this would save my marriage.
Day 90 came.
I felt proud of myself.
Tried to have sex with my wife.
Nothing.
Maybe I needed more time.
Did another 26 days.
Still nothing.
That's when the real damage started.
Because every time I couldn't perform, my wife saw it differently than I did.
I thought: "I broke myself with porn."
She thought: "He's not attracted to me anymore."
I started avoiding intimacy because I was ashamed.
She stopped initiating because she thought I didn't want her.
I'd wear sweatpants to bed.
She stopped wearing anything that showed her body.
We'd lie there in the dark, both feeling rejected, both thinking the other person had moved on.
The distance became unbearable.
She started going to bed earlier.
I started staying up later.
We'd have entire weekends without touching.
The coldness in our house was suffocating.
One night after another failed attempt, she got up to leave the room.
I asked where she was going.
She stopped at the door and said something that broke me:
"I don't know why you're even trying anymore. We both know you're not into this."
That's when I realized.
She thought I didn't want her.
All this time, I'd been carrying guilt about breaking myself with porn.
And she'd been carrying guilt about not being enough for me.
Neither of us was right.
Here's where everything changed...
I finally went to my doctor.
Told him the whole story.
The porn.
The NoFap.
The shame.
The distance with my wife.
He listened.
Then he said something that made me want to punch a wall.
"Porn might affect your arousal for about 30 minutes after you finish. That's it. It doesn't permanently rewire anything. But you know what does destroy erections? Aging blood vessels. After your mid-thirties, your nitric oxide production collapses. That's the molecule that opens your blood vessels and gets you hard. No nitric oxide, no blood flow, no erection. Doesn't matter how attracted you are. Your circulation is failing. This isn't about porn. It's about your cardiovascular system giving you an early warning."
I just sat there.
"So you're telling me I spent three years thinking I broke myself... and my wife spent three years thinking I wasn't attracted to her... and it was just my fucking circulation?"
"Exactly. And if you don't fix it, it gets worse. This vessel collapse is one of the earliest signs of heart disease."
I didn't know whether to feel relieved or furious.
Years of guilt.
Years of my wife thinking she wasn't beautiful anymore.
Years of avoiding intimacy because we were both terrified of rejection.
And it wasn't even the porn.
He broke down the mechanism.
When circulation starts failing, your body experiences what he called Nitric Oxide Collapse:
-Hormonal Shift: Your hormones start cutting off nitric oxide production at the source.
-Cellular Energy Drain: Your blood vessels lose the energy to expand, relax, and actually keep blood where it needs to be.
Porn doesn't touch either of those systems.
A failing cardiovascular system does.

The wife’s shocking sexual confession.
Catherine almost didn't agree to sit down for this.

She laughed, a little embarrassed.
"My friends think I'm lying. Then they get angry. One of them actually asked me what my husband is on."
Here's the thing.
He isn't on anything a doctor prescribed.

THE MAN WHO WALKED AWAY FROM $340,000 A YEAR
Catherine's husband is Donald Mitchell.
For almost two decades, Donald was a senior scientist inside Pfizer — the same company that invented Viagra.
He spent his career in their vascular division, studying exactly how blood moves through the aging male body.
In 2003, he quit.
No gold watch.
No retirement party.
He just handed in his badge and walked.
During his final sit-down with HR, he said one sentence that never made it into any company record: "We already figured out how to actually fix this. We're just not allowed to sell it, because nobody can put a patent on nature."
He was talking about erectile dysfunction.
And what he knew is the reason he and Catherine are still living like newlyweds nearly twenty years later.
WHAT PFIZER FOUND — AND SHOVED IN A DRAWER
Donald's team had one job: figure out why Viagra was a miracle for some men and completely useless for others.
The pill works by muscling your blood vessels open for a short window.
Force.
Pressure.
Brute strength.
But in the trials, somewhere between a third and half of the men got nothing.
Zero.
The drug pried their vessels open and blood still refused to flow.
Donald wanted to know why.
What his team uncovered came down to a single molecule almost nobody talks about: nitric oxide.
Think of nitric oxide as the messenger that tells your blood vessels to loosen, widen, and let blood pour in on its own.
No force required.
It's how your body worked effortlessly when you were 25.
Then time gets involved.
Past 40, your production drops about 10% every year.
By your mid-fifties, you've lost close to half.
By 65, you're barely making any at all.
When that messenger goes silent, your vessels clamp shut.
Blood can't get where it needs to go.
Not to your groin.
Not to your brain.
Not to your hands and feet.
Viagra tries to kick the door down.
But if the real problem is that nobody's sending the signal, kicking harder does nothing.
That's why the pill fades over the years — and why so many men still lose their erection halfway through, even with a pill in their system.
THE STUDY THAT GOT KILLED IN 48 HOURS
Donald's team noticed something they weren't supposed to.
Men who got their nitric oxide back responded far better to treatment.
And most of them stopped needing the pill entirely once their bodies started producing the molecule again.
So Donald pitched a simple idea: restore a man's nitric oxide first, naturally, and see how many even need a prescription afterward.
Two days later, the proposal was dead.
"A director told me flat out — there's no money in curing it," Donald recalled.
"Viagra was pulling in billions a year. Teach men to fix this at the source and that river of cash dries up overnight."
So Donald kept researching.
On his own time.
On his own dime.
He dug through endocrinology papers, small clinical trials, and vascular studies most physicians never read.
Every road led to the same place: You can stop what he calls Nitric Oxide Collapse — and switch your blood flow back on — using a handful of specific natural compounds.
The logic is almost stupidly simple.
Stop trying to s

When Catherine agreed to sit down for this interview, she revealed the one secret that makes all of her friends fuming with jealousy about her thriving sex life with her husband.
"Most of our friends stopped having sex in their 60s," Catherine said with a guilty smile, like she knew she was lucky but wouldn’t trade it for the world. "Their husbands are on pills that barely work. My husband is 71 — and we still do it 2-3 times a day. Yes… per day. Just like when we were 20!"
Her  husband is a man named Dr. Donald Mitchell. In 2003, he walked away from a $340,000 salary and 19 years at Pfizer, shocking all of his colleagues. In his exit interview, he admitted something that should terrify every man taking ED pills: "We know what actually restores erectile function. We're just not allowed to sell it because you can't patent a natural solution."
Today, Donald is 71 years old. He hasn't taken Viagra in 18 years.
"I honestly didn't think we'd still have this part of our marriage at our age," Catherine admitted. "Donald does something different than every other husband in our circle. It really keeps life exciting."
What Donald discovered during his time at Pfizer changed everything he thought he knew about erectile dysfunction. And it has nothing to do with the blue pill.
The Pfizer Files: What They Found (And Buried)
During Donald's tenure in Pfizer's vascular research division, the team was tasked with understanding why Viagra worked for some men but failed for others.
The drug worked by forcing blood vessels to dilate temporarily. But in clinical trials, 30-40% of men saw zero improvement. The drug forced their vessels open, but blood still wouldn't flow properly.
Donald's team discovered why: These men had depleted levels of a molecule called nitric oxide.
Nitric oxide is the molecule that signals your blood vessels to relax, open up, and fill with blood naturally. After age 40, your body produces roughly 10% less every year. By 55, you've lost nearly half of what you had at 30. By 65, you're running on fumes.
Without nitric oxide, your blood vessels stay constricted. Blood can't flow freely. Not to your penis. Not to your brain. Not anywhere.
Viagra tries to force the plumbing open. But if you are experiencing Nitric Oxide Collapse, the signal never gets sent. It's like trying to open a door by pushing harder when the lock is broken. That's why it stops working over time and why men still go soft halfway through, even on the pill.
The Discovery Pfizer Refused to Pursue
Donald's team found that men with restored nitric oxide levels responded dramatically better to ED treatment. Most didn't need the pill at all once their nitric oxide production was reactivated.
So Donald proposed a study: What if we help men restore nitric oxide naturally before prescribing Viagra?
The proposal was rejected within 48 hours.
"They told me there's no business model in solving the problem," Donald said in a 2019 interview. "Viagra generates $2 billion annually. If men restore nitric oxide naturally and don't need the drug anymore, that revenue disappears."
Donald kept digging on his own time. He found research from natural endocrinologists, clinical trials, and vascular studies. All pointing to the same breakthrough: Your body can stop Nitric Oxide Collapse and reactivate blood flow by using specific, naturally occurring compounds.
Here's the mechanism: Instead of forcing blood through a broken system, you can supply your body with exact ingredients specially made for a man to restore his nitric oxide production directly at the source.
The research proved it works: Specific adaptogens drop the hormones that cut off nitric oxide, while others rebuild cellular energy and sustain blood flow.
And when your body has the nitric oxide it needs… Blood vessels relax naturally. Blood flow returns to normal. Erections happen the way they're supposed to.
No forced dilation. No pill windows. No side effects. Just your body working the way it did when you were 30

The men on tamsulosin all tell me some version of the same thing.
The stream’s better during the day. They’re still up three times a night. And lately, when they stand up at 3am, the room doesn’t stand still with them.
Their dose went up at the last check-up. The trips to the bathroom didn’t come down.
Every visit ends the same way. A glance at the flow, a refill, and one word: “age.”
I’m Veda. I’ve been a nurse for 32 years, and that one word is where most men get left behind.
Here’s what’s really happening.
Tamsulosin loosens the muscle where the bladder empties. For ten, twelve hours the pressure lets up and the stream picks up. That part is real. I’m not going to tell you it isn’t.
But it loosens blood vessels too. Get up fast at 3am, half asleep, on a tablet like that, and a lot of men feel the floor shift. They reach for the doorframe. They keep it from their wives. If that’s you, call your doctor this week. Don’t wait for the next review.
And here’s the part the review never checks.
The flow is only what you can see. Underneath, the gland keeps growing.
From about 40, the body turns more of its testosterone into a hormone called DHT. DHT gets into the prostate, locks onto the tissue, and tells it to keep growing. So the gland swells and presses harder on the exit, year after year.
Two steps. The body makes too much DHT. Then the DHT locks on.
The tablet was never built to touch either one.
So the bladder never fully empties. It’s full again by one. Up. Dizzy. Back to bed. Full again by three.
Better flow by day, same nights. That’s the tell.
Which is why the usual advice does so little.
Cutting water after six shrinks what goes in, not what’s left behind.
Pharmacy saw palmetto only works on the first step. It slows how much DHT gets made. It does nothing about the DHT already locked onto the gland.
Most pumpkin seed products are heat-processed powders at 250 to 500mg. The heat destroys the very sterols that do the work.
A higher dose of the tablet relaxes the same muscle harder. It still doesn’t reach the DHT. For some men it just means a worse head-rush on the way to the bathroom.
What finally helped the men I know went after both steps at once.
Saw palmetto to slow how much DHT the body makes. Cold-pressed pumpkin seed oil, rich in beta-sitosterol and delta-7 sterols, to block what’s left from locking onto the gland. Cold-pressed, so the sterols survive. 1,000mg combined in two softgels a day, taken alongside whatever their doctor prescribed.
Many of them tell me the same thing around week three.
One less trip.
Then a night they only get up once. Which means one walk down a dark hallway on a dizzy head instead of three.
Then their wife mentions they’ve stopped nodding off in the chair after lunch.
It’s called Elivora Pumpkin Seed Oil. It has a 30-day money-back guarantee, and if it starts working, give it the full three months. That’s when the nights really settle.
If any of this sounds familiar, the article below explains why the flow gets better and the nights don’t, and what to do about the part the review never measures.
Link below.
PS. Tonight, when you get up, sit on the edge of the bed and count to ten before you stand. Then count how many times you do it before morning. That second number is the one your review should be asking about.

There are some side effects of pumpkin seed oil that you should be warned about before you start taking it.
Your nighttime bathroom trips may suddenly drop from four or five down to one within the first 8 weeks. You may finally empty your bladder completely for the first time in years. You may start sleeping through the night without waking up. Your stream may return to the strength you remember from your forties. Your wife may stop asking why you’re up again at 3 a.m. because you’re no longer getting up.
These changes have been reported consistently among men over 50 who bring their DHT back under control over 8 to 12 weeks. If you are comfortable with the way your body has been feeling lately, this product is not for you.
If you are not, keep reading.
What you are feeling is not normal aging. It is the slow buildup of a hormone called DHT inside your prostate. When you were younger, your body kept DHT in check. After 40, an enzyme called 5-alpha-reductase starts turning more and more of your testosterone into DHT. And DHT is what tells your prostate to keep growing.
As DHT builds, your prostate swells and squeezes the tube that carries urine out of your body. Your stream weakens. You wake up two, three, four times a night. You never feel fully empty. And every standard medication your doctor prescribes is treating one symptom while the gland itself keeps growing.
In 1995, The Lancet, one of the most respected medical journals in the world, published a study on 200 men with enlarged prostates. The men given a plant sterol called beta-sitosterol saw their urinary symptoms improve and their flow get stronger, while the placebo group barely changed. Beta-sitosterol works by blocking DHT from latching onto your prostate cells.
The richest natural source of beta-sitosterol and delta-7 sterols, the sterols that do this, is cold-pressed pumpkin seed oil. Pair it with saw palmetto, which slows the enzyme that makes DHT in the first place, and less DHT gets made while what’s left can’t latch on.
The catch is that these sterols are fragile. Almost every pumpkin seed supplement on the market is heat-extracted, underdosed at 250 to 500mg, and missing saw palmetto entirely. The heat destroys most of the sterols before they ever reach the capsule. That’s why most prostate supplements feel like they do nothing.
Elivora’s Pumpkin Seed Oil solves this. 1,000mg of cold-pressed pumpkin seed oil and saw palmetto per serving, never heated, so the beta-sitosterol and delta-7 sterols stay intact, sealed in a softgel so they reach your prostate the way nature made them.
Two softgels every morning with breakfast. One bottle is a 30-day supply.
A 30-day money-back guarantee means you have a full month to see whether your body actually responds. If it doesn’t, send the bottle back, even empty, and the company refunds every penny.
You can keep blaming the night trips, the weak stream, and the constant urge to go on getting older.
Or you can address what’s actually causing them.

My husband skipped tamsulosin for 4 years. His doctor was shocked by the ultrasound.
I’ve been a nurse for 28 years. I’ve catheterized men who couldn’t empty their bladders. I’ve watched prostates grow so large that surgery was the only way they could urinate. And I’ve seen the same men come back every three months with the same complaints—urgency, weak stream, getting up four, five, six times a night—while their doctors simply adjusted the dose and said, “Give it time.”
Then my husband started getting up every ninety minutes.
And I realized “give it time” was the most dangerous phrase in urology.
I need to tell you about my father first. Because until you understand what I watched happen to him, nothing else I’m about to say will make sense.
My father was diagnosed with an enlarged prostate at 58. His doctor put him on tamsulosin immediately—0.4 milligrams, once daily. He was the perfect patient. Never missed a dose. Cut out coffee. Stopped drinking after 7 p.m. Did everything they told him.
His symptoms improved slightly. His urologist smiled at the flow test and said, “Looking better, Frank. Stay the course.”
He stayed the course for eleven years.
Eleven years of tamsulosin.
Eleven years of “manageable symptoms.”
Eleven years of his doctor looking at a chart and calling it a success.
But the side effects were never manageable. The dizziness started in year two—he couldn’t stand up without grabbing the wall. The fatigue was constant. He’d fall asleep in his recliner at 6 p.m. like someone had unplugged him. And the one thing he never talked about—the thing my mother only told me after he was gone—was that the medication destroyed their intimacy. Completely. For the last eight years of his life, they slept in the same bed like roommates.
And his prostate kept growing.
Tamsulosin doesn’t shrink the prostate. It doesn’t stop whatever is causing the growth. It simply relaxes the muscles around the bladder so urine can squeeze past the obstruction. It’s like putting a bigger doorframe around a door that’s still being blocked. The obstruction never moves. It just gets bigger. Eventually, the bigger doorframe isn’t enough.
At 67, my father couldn’t urinate at all. Acute urinary retention. They rushed him to the ER and inserted a catheter. He came home with a bag strapped to his leg. For five weeks, a man who built his own deck and coached Little League walked around with a catheter bag under his pants. He stopped going to church. He stopped going to the hardware store. He sat in his recliner and watched the bag fill.
The TURP surgery was supposed to fix everything. They scraped out the prostate tissue blocking his urethra. The surgeon called it “routine.” My father came home wearing adult diapers. The incontinence was supposed to be temporary. It wasn’t.
He leaked for the rest of his life. Every cough. Every laugh. Every time he stood up from a chair. The man who built things with his hands was now carrying spare underwear in a plastic bag whenever he left the house.
He stopped leaving the house.
He died at 71. Not from the prostate. From pneumonia. But I’ll tell you what really killed him—it was the giving up. A man who quits going outside, quits moving, quits seeing friends because he’s terrified of leaking through his pants in public… that man’s immune system collapses. My father didn’t die of pneumonia. He died of eleven years on a medication that masked the symptom while the actual problem destroyed his body from the inside.
Three years ago, my husband Robert started getting up at night. Once at first. Then twice. Then four times. Then every ninety minutes like clockwork.
I’d lie in bed listening to him get up. The mattress shifting. His feet on the floor. The bathroom light clicking on. The stream that took thirty seconds to start and another thirty to finish. The flush. The mattress shifting again. And then, forty-five minutes later, all of it again.
His doctor examined him. Digital rectal exam. PSA. Ultrasound. “Robert, you

What you are feeling is not normal aging. It is the slow buildup of a hormone called DHT inside your prostate. When you were younger, your body kept DHT in check. After 40, an enzyme called 5-alpha-reductase starts turning more and more of your testosterone into DHT. And DHT is what tells your prostate to keep growing.
As DHT builds, your prostate swells and squeezes the tube that carries urine out of your body. Your stream weakens. You wake up two, three, four times a night. You never feel fully empty. And every standard medication your doctor prescribes is treating one symptom while the gland itself keeps growing.
In 1995, The Lancet, one of the most respected medical journals in the world, published a study on 200 men with enlarged prostates. The men given a plant sterol called beta-sitosterol saw their urinary symptoms improve and their flow get stronger, while the placebo group barely changed. Beta-sitosterol works by blocking DHT from latching onto your prostate cells.
The richest natural source of beta-sitosterol and delta-7 sterols, the sterols that do this, is cold-pressed pumpkin seed oil. Pair it with saw palmetto, which slows the enzyme that makes DHT in the first place, and less DHT gets made while what’s left can’t latch on.
The catch is that these sterols are fragile. Almost every pumpkin seed supplement on the market is heat-extracted, underdosed at 250 to 500mg, and missing saw palmetto entirely. The heat destroys most of the sterols before they ever reach the capsule. That’s why most prostate supplements feel like they do nothing.
Elivora’s Pumpkin Seed Oil solves this. 1,000mg of cold-pressed pumpkin seed oil and saw palmetto per serving, never heated, so the beta-sitosterol and delta-7 sterols stay intact, sealed in a softgel so they reach your prostate the way nature made them.

There are some side effects of pumpkin seed oil that you should be warned about before you start taking it.
Your nighttime bathroom trips may suddenly drop from four or five down to one within the first 8 weeks. You may finally empty your bladder completely for the first time in years. You may start sleeping through the night without waking up. Your stream may return to the strength you remember from your forties. Your wife may stop asking why you’re up again at 3 a.m. because you’re no longer getting up.
These changes have been reported consistently among men over 50 who bring their DHT back under control over 8 to 12 weeks. If you are comfortable with the way your body has been feeling lately, this product is not for you.
If you are not, keep reading.
What you are feeling is not normal aging. It is the slow buildup of a hormone called DHT inside your prostate. When you were younger, your body kept DHT in check. After 40, an enzyme called 5-alpha-reductase starts turning more and more of your testosterone into DHT. And DHT is what tells your prostate to keep growing.
As DHT builds, your prostate swells and squeezes the tube that carries urine out of your body. Your stream weakens. You wake up two, three, four times a night. You never feel fully empty. And every standard medication your doctor prescribes is treating one symptom while the gland itself keeps growing.
In 1995, The Lancet, one of the most respected medical journals in the world, published a study on 200 men with enlarged prostates. The men given a plant sterol called beta-sitosterol saw their urinary symptoms improve and their flow get stronger, while the placebo group barely changed. Beta-sitosterol works by blocking DHT from latching onto your prostate cells.
The richest natural source of beta-sitosterol and delta-7 sterols, the sterols that do this, is cold-pressed pumpkin seed oil. Pair it with saw palmetto, which slows the enzyme that makes DHT in the first place, and less DHT gets made while what’s left can’t latch on.
The catch is that these sterols are fragile. Almost every pumpkin seed supplement on the market is heat-extracted, underdosed at 250 to 500mg, and missing saw palmetto entirely. The heat destroys most of the sterols before they ever reach the capsule. That’s why most prostate supplements feel like they do nothing.
Elivora’s Pumpkin Seed Oil solves this. 1,000mg of cold-pressed pumpkin seed oil and saw palmetto per serving, never heated, so the beta-sitosterol and delta-7 sterols stay intact, sealed in a softgel so they reach your prostate the way nature made them.
Two softgels every morning with breakfast. One bottle is a 30-day supply.
A 30-day money-back guarantee means you have a full month to see whether your body actually responds. If it doesn’t, send the bottle back, even empty, and the company refunds every penny.
You can keep blaming the night trips, the weak stream, and the constant urge to go on getting older.
Or you can address what’s actually causing them.

Something that’s going to make every urologist, family doctor, and supplement company very, very angry.
I’ve been told to stay in my lane. Told that I’m “just a urologist” and I should leave the science to the professionals.
But I don’t care anymore.
Not after watching men I’ve known for years sit across from me white as a ghost because of a number on a blood test that nobody explained to them.
I see it every single week.
A man walks in. His doctor ran routine bloodwork. His PSA came back elevated. Maybe 4. Maybe 6. Maybe 9. And now he thinks he has cancer.
His wife is crying in the waiting room. He hasn’t slept in three days. He’s already googled survival rates. He’s already looked at his grandkids differently.
And in most cases, not all, but most, I run the tests, I do the workup, and I tell him:

The relief in that room is something I’ll never get used to.

He’d read about the side effects.
The libido issues.
The brain fog.
He’d already been through the worst scare of his life. He wasn’t about to trade it for a drug that made him feel like half a man.
He told me, very politely, that he’d rather wake up four times a night than risk what those drugs do to a man’s body.
I tried to talk him through the data. He wasn’t having it. He left without filling the prescription.

There are currently 847 prostate supplements listed on Amazon. I know because I counted. Every single one of them claims to support prostate health, urinary flow, and fewer nighttime trips to the bathroom. Fewer than a handful are formulated in a way that allows the active compounds to reach the prostate tissue making those claims possible. Here is exactly what separates the ones that work from the 840+ that do not.
My name is Greg. I am a co-founder of Elivora.
I counted those 847 products on a Tuesday afternoon in January after the third email that week from a customer who had tried 3 other prostate supplements before finding us. All 3 had done nothing. They had concluded saw palmetto did not work. That pumpkin seed did not work. They had almost not ordered Elivora. And then they went from 4 trips a night to 1 in 4 weeks, and the stream they had lost years ago came back, and they were writing to ask why everything they had tried before had failed when ours had not.
I owe them an honest answer. And I owe it to every man who has tried a prostate supplement and concluded it does not work. Because the conclusion is wrong. The products were wrong. The mechanism is real and the delivery is what is broken in almost everything making the claim.
Here is what the mechanism is and why 4 specific variables determine whether any product on that list actually delivers it.
Inside your prostate, an enzyme called 5-alpha-reductase converts testosterone into DHT. DHT is the hormone that tells prostate cells to keep growing. It locks onto receptors in the prostate tissue and delivers that signal every hour of every day, for decades, and it never switches off on its own. The gland wraps around the urethra, the tube that drains your bladder. It keeps getting the signal to grow. It swells. It squeezes. The stream weakens. The bladder never fully empties. The 2am and 4am trips begin. Stopping this takes 2 things happening at the same time. Saw palmetto slows the enzyme, so less testosterone is converted into DHT in the first place. Beta-sitosterol and delta-7 sterols, the active compounds in pumpkin seed oil, occupy the receptors in prostate tissue so the DHT already there has nowhere to land. Less DHT made. Less DHT landing. The growth signal goes quiet. The squeeze on the urethra eases. The stream comes back. The nights go quiet. This is the mechanism every 1 of those 847 products is claiming to deliver. Here is why 840-plus of them cannot.
The first and largest failure is delivery format. Beta-sitosterol and delta-7 sterols are fat-soluble. They live in the oil of the pumpkin seed, not in the fiber, not in the protein. When a manufacturer grinds the seed into a powder, or uses pumpkin seed meal left over after the oil has already been pressed out and sold, the sterols are either a fraction of what they should be or gone entirely. And fat-soluble compounds swallowed in a dry powder capsule have no fat to carry them across the intestinal wall. What reaches the prostate is a trace. The receptors in prostate tissue never receive a meaningful dose. The DHT keeps landing. Nothing changes at 3am. This single variable eliminates approximately 90% of the prostate supplement market before anything else is considered.
For the sterols to work, they have to arrive in oil. Oil, not powder. So they absorb with the fat they were built to travel in. But how the oil is made matters too. Most manufacturers who do use an oil format extract it with high heat or chemical solvents because both pull more oil from every seed and both cost less per bottle. Heat breaks down beta-sitosterol and delta-7 sterols. The compounds the product exists to deliver are damaged before the oil is ever bottled. The label still says pumpkin seed oil. The oil no longer does what pumpkin seed oil is supposed to do. The correct oil is cold-pressed. Extracted without high heat. Sterols intact. Most oil-based products in this category fail here. Of the small number that survive the delivery

I took saw palmetto because I was scared of finasteride side effects.
8 months later I was getting up more than when I started. Because it was only ever doing half the job.
I need to tell you this story because nobody warned me. Not the label. Not the website. Not the man at the health food store who said “it’s natural, it’s safe, it’s been used for centuries.” Not my doctor who shrugged and said “can’t hurt to try.” Not a single article in 6 months of Googling “natural prostate supplement that actually works.”
Nobody told me saw palmetto only does half the job. Nobody told me what the other half was. Nobody told me that the “safe natural alternative” I was taking to avoid the prescription was guarding one door while the thing growing my prostate walked straight through the other.
I found out the way you find out everything the supplement industry doesn’t want you to know: by living inside the damage and working backward.
I was 54. Up 3 times a night. Stream slowing down month by month. Dribbling at the end. The kind of changes you tell yourself are just age until they’re not just age anymore.
My doctor mentioned finasteride. Casually. Like he was suggesting a restaurant. “We could try finasteride. It targets the hormone driving the growth. Very effective.”
I went home and looked it up.
“Lost sensation. Libido gone. Erections that don’t hold. I stopped the pill 3 years ago. Nothing’s come back.”
That was one man. 3 years after stopping finasteride. Still broken.
“We went from 4 times a week to once if I’m lucky. He says it’s fine. It’s not fine. I’m scared it’s permanent.”
That was a woman writing about her husband. 8 months on finasteride. She was watching him disappear.
“Fin killed my drive. Just gone.”
“Why am I dealing with all these sides AND still getting up at night? Isn’t this supposed to fix the prostate?”
I read those posts at midnight sitting on the edge of my bed after my 3rd trip to the bathroom and I decided I would never take finasteride. Not if I was up 10 times a night. Not if my stream stopped completely. Not if they wheeled me into surgery. I would not take a drug that could permanently alter the part of me that made me feel like a man.
So I did what millions of men do. I bought the “safe” version.
Saw palmetto. The natural alternative. The one every health food store puts at eye level. The one every prostate supplement on the shelf is built around. The one that says “supports prostate health” in green letters on a brown bottle and feels like you’re doing something responsible without risking anything.
I took it every morning for 8 months.
Months 1 and 2: nothing. Not better, not worse. I told myself it was building up. That’s what the website said. “Allow 6 to 8 weeks for full effect.” I allowed.
Month 3: still nothing. Same trips. Same trickle. Same dribble. But something else was happening that I couldn’t name yet. I was planning my life around a bathroom. Aisle seats. No coffee after noon. Finding the restroom sign in every restaurant before I sat down. Not a decision I made. Just something I started doing without noticing.
I blamed age. I blamed coffee. I blamed stress.
Month 4: the trips went from 3 to 4. Quietly. Not a cliff. A slope. I’d lie there at 2 AM listening to my wife breathe, doing the math on how many hours I had left before the alarm, knowing I’d be up again before I got there.
I still didn’t connect it.
Month 5: the dribble got worse. I’d finish, shake, zip, and feel the warm spot spread on my khakis 10 seconds later. In a restaurant. Across the table from my grandson. The kind of thing you notice and stare at for a second and think “huh” and then pull your shirt down over it because you don’t have anyone to ask and you don’t have the vocabulary to describe it.
Month 6: my wife reached for me on a Saturday morning and I flinched. Not because I didn’t want her. Because I’d been up 4 times that night, I was running on fumes, and part of my brain was already wondering if I’d have to get up i

Since we launched Elivora, products have appeared on Amazon, at Walmart, at pharmacies, at supplement stores and e-commerce sites across the internet, all of them claiming pumpkin seed oil and saw palmetto in softgels, all of them packaged and labeled closely enough to what we make that our customers could not tell the difference from the outside.
When the comments started arriving that pumpkin seed oil was not working, that men had been taking softgels for weeks and were still getting up three and four times a night, I said nothing publicly. I thought the product would speak for itself. It did not. People blamed Elivora. But the product they were holding was not Elivora.
My name is Greg. I am a co-founder of Elivora. What I am about to explain is the answer to a question we receive more than any other, from more people than I expected: why does Elivora say two softgels per day when almost every other pumpkin seed product on the market tells you to take one or three dry capsules?
The answer is what this post is about. And once you understand it, you will not need to wait another month to figure out why you are still getting up the same number of times every night.
---
The comment pattern
The question arrives in different forms but carries the same confusion.
Someone writes that he is taking a pumpkin seed capsule and the label says to take three per day. Elivora says take two softgels. He wants to know which is correct. He will find out after another month of broken nights.
Someone else writes that his also says three capsules per day. What is the right dose?
Another man writes that his product’s back label is nearly identical to Elivora’s. Same claims, same mechanism language, same described delivery. But the product comes in a hard capsule, not a softgel.
Another writes that he takes saw palmetto on its own and it clearly says standardized to 85% fatty acids, and he wants to know why Elivora bothers pairing it with pumpkin seed oil.
These are not isolated comments. They arrive every day, from men in different countries, from every corner of the internet where Elivora appears. The volume of the question is significant in itself. It means there are dozens of products circulating in this category making claims similar enough to ours that our customers cannot reliably distinguish them from the label alone. It means those products are directing men to take one, two, or three dry capsules per day. And it means the men taking them are waiting for results that are not arriving.
The dosage difference is not an accident. It is the direct consequence of a formulation decision. And the formulation decision is what determines whether a man sleeps through to 6am, whether the stream comes without waiting for it, whether the drive to see his grandkids still needs two rest stops. Or whether all of it stays exactly where it has been for the last three urologist appointments.
---
What we found when we investigated
When the comment pattern became clear, I began ordering competing products. Not one or two. Dozens. From Amazon. From Walmart. From online supplement retailers. From pharmacy platforms. Every product I could find that claimed to be pumpkin seed oil, every product using the same mechanism language Elivora uses, every product priced in a range that made the formulation cost difficult to explain.
We brought everything to our laboratory team.
The analysis took weeks. We tested what was actually inside every product: whether it was cold-pressed pumpkin seed oil or dry ground pumpkin seed powder, and how much. We tested the oil itself: whether it had been pressed cold or extracted with heat, and whether it had been cut with a cheaper filler oil. We checked for saw palmetto, and at what amount. We measured the total milligrams per recommended daily serving and compared that figure to the dose the research used.
What we found across the majority of competing products confirmed what the pricing had already suggested. The label said softgel or

If you're a man over 50 dealing with:
🚽 Constant trips to the bathroom (especially at night)
💧 A weak stream that starts, stops, and won't finish

Since we launched Elivora, products have appeared on Amazon, at Walmart, at pharmacies, at supplement stores and e-commerce sites across the internet, all of them claiming pumpkin seed oil and saw palmetto in softgels, all of them packaged and labeled closely enough to what we make that our customers could not tell the difference from the outside. Since we launched Elivora, products have appeared on Amazon, at Walmart, at pharmacies, at supplement stores and e-commerce sites across the internet, all of them claiming pumpkin seed oil and saw palmetto in softgels, all of them packaged and labeled closely enough to what we make that our customers could not tell the difference from the outside.
When the comments started arriving that pumpkin seed oil was not working, that men had been taking softgels for weeks and were still getting up three and four times a night, I said nothing publicly. I thought the product would speak for itself. It did not. People blamed Elivora. But the product they were holding was not Elivora.
My name is Greg. I am a co-founder of Elivora. What I am about to explain is the answer to a question we receive more than any other, from more people than I expected: why does Elivora say two softgels per day when almost every other pumpkin seed product on the market tells you to take one or three dry capsules?
The answer is what this post is about. And once you understand it, you will not need to wait another month to figure out why you are still getting up the same number of times every night.
---
The comment pattern
The question arrives in different forms but carries the same confusion.
Someone writes that he is taking a pumpkin seed capsule and the label says to take three per day. Elivora says take two softgels. He wants to know which is correct. He will find out after another month of broken nights.
Someone else writes that his also says three capsules per day. What is the right dose?
Another man writes that his product’s back label is nearly identical to Elivora’s. Same claims, same mechanism language, same described delivery. But the product comes in a hard capsule, not a softgel.
Another writes that he takes saw palmetto on its own and it clearly says standardized to 85% fatty acids, and he wants to know why Elivora bothers pairing it with pumpkin seed oil.
These are not isolated comments. They arrive every day, from men in different countries, from every corner of the internet where Elivora appears. The volume of the question is significant in itself. It means there are dozens of products circulating in this category making claims similar enough to ours that our customers cannot reliably distinguish them from the label alone. It means those products are directing men to take one, two, or three dry capsules per day. And it means the men taking them are waiting for results that are not arriving.
The dosage difference is not an accident. It is the direct consequence of a formulation decision. And the formulation decision is what determines whether a man sleeps through to 6am, whether the stream comes without waiting for it, whether the drive to see his grandkids still needs two rest stops. Or whether all of it stays exactly where it has been for the last three urologist appointments.
---
What we found when we investigated
When the comment pattern became clear, I began ordering competing products. Not one or two. Dozens. From Amazon. From Walmart. From online supplement retailers. From pharmacy platforms. Every product I could find that claimed to be pumpkin seed oil, every product using the same mechanism language Elivora uses, every product priced in a range that made the formulation cost difficult to explain.
We brought everything to our laboratory team.
The analysis took weeks. We tested what was actually inside every product: whether it was cold-pressed pumpkin seed oil or dry ground pumpkin seed powder, and how much. We tested the oil itself: whether it had been pressed cold or extracted with heat, and whether it had been cut with a cheaper filler oil. We checked for saw palmetto, and at what amount. We measured the total milligrams per recommended daily serving and compared that figure to the dose the research used.
What we found across the majority of competing products confirmed what the pricing had already suggested. The label said softgel or

This month we received over 800 refund requests, which is more than we have received in any single month since we launched Elivora. I want to be completely transparent with you about what is actually behind that number, because the first reaction most people have when they hear it is to assume the product is failing. It is not. This month we received over 800 refund requests, which is more than we have received in any single month since we launched Elivora.
I want to be completely transparent with you about what is actually behind that number, because the first reaction most people have when they hear it is to assume the product is failing. It is not. The breakdown of where those refund requests came from is the most important thing you will read about our company this year.
94 percent of those 800 refund requests came from people who bought what they thought was Elivora on Amazon.
2 percent came from people who bought from third-party sellers or websites impersonating our brand.
2 percent came from shipping delays affecting real orders from our official site.
2 percent came from real customers of our real product who did not see the results they were hoping for.
So out of more than 800 refund requests this month, only about 16 were from men who actually purchased Elivora from us and did not feel the product worked for them. 16 refund requests in a month, against thousands of real orders, is something we are honestly proud of.
The other 784 are a problem we did not create and cannot fully control, but which every potential customer of ours needs to understand before they buy.
We have never sold Elivora’s Pumpkin Seed Oil on Amazon. We have never authorised any third-party reseller. The only place Elivora exists for legitimate sale is on elivorahealth.com.
But our success over the last year has made us a target. Counterfeiters have been listing fake Elivora on Amazon at prices below our official site, using stolen product images, fabricated reviews, and packaging designed to look almost identical to ours. People search for our brand, see what appears to be our product at a discount, assume they are buying from us, and end up with cheap pumpkin seed capsules containing none of the cold-pressed oil, none of the saw palmetto, none of the formulation that makes our product actually work.
When the fake product does nothing and they are still getting up three times a night, those men come to us asking for refunds. They send us order confirmations from warehouses we have never shipped from. They send us packaging photos that do not match our real bottles. They send us shipping labels with our brand name but tracking numbers we never generated.
We cannot refund orders we never processed. We have been telling people this all month. Some of them are understandably furious, and the worst part is that almost all of their anger should be directed at Amazon for hosting these listings in the first place, not at us. But we are the brand on the label, and we are the ones taking the calls.
I am writing this because you might be the next person about to make the same mistake.
If you are considering Elivora and your first instinct is to check Amazon for the best price, please do not. The listings you find there are not us. The product you receive will not be the real formulation. The refund you request will go to a counterfeiter who will ignore you, not to a real company that honours its guarantee.
The only place to buy real Elivora’s Pumpkin Seed Oil is elivorahealth.com. Every bottle ordered from our official site is the real product, formulated correctly, sealed to protect the oil from light and air, with the full 1,000mg of cold-pressed pumpkin seed oil and saw palmetto per serving that our formula and our results are built on.
Every order placed on our site is backed by our 30-day money-back guarantee. If the product does not work for you, we will refund you. That promise has not changed and will not change. But the promise only applies to real product purchased from the real source.
If you are serious about giving the protocol an honest trial, the easiest way to do that is to order directly from our website, take your two softgels every day for the full 30 days, and let your body show you whether it works. If it does not, the bottle goes back and the money returns

Natural Health & Wellness Club · 🖼 image · débutstarted 17 oct 2026 · depuis 10 jrunning 10 days · rang 9 dans la pagerank 9 on the page
If you're on Tamsulosin, you should know I'm a urologist who refused it for my own prostate after prescribing it to thousands of men. If you're on Tamsulosin, you should know I'm a urologist who refused it for my own prostate after prescribing it to thousands of men. Its most faithful takers kept coming back in pads, and at 54, up four times a night, I wasn't going to be next.
I've seen too many prostate scans to stay quiet about this any longer.
In 25 years, I've carried out more than 3,000 prostate procedures. And when a man comes to me already on his second or third dose increase, I already know exactly where he's heading.
Another 18 to 24 months, and he ends up in the ER: emergency catheterization, a bag strapped to his leg, then surgery.
My name is Greg Ellison, consultant urologist, 25 years in practice.
I still carry out TURP and HoLEP procedures, but only when there is genuinely no other option: kidney damage, full urinary retention, men in their eighties whose prostates have been allowed to keep growing unchecked for decades.
Every day I see men in their sixties being sent to the operating room when it never had to come to that.
And I was part of the very system that sent them there.
I've been prescribing Tamsulosin for 25 years, and I've seen thousands of men start taking it, thousands who felt a bit of relief and thought their problem was finally under control. And I've seen what happens 3, 4, 5 years later when they come back. The 63-year-old who took his Tamsulosin faithfully for three years never missed a dose, did everything his urologist told him, and ended up in the ER at three in the morning with 900 milliliters of urine trapped in his bladder that simply would not come out. A catheter went through his urethra, his wife crying in the corner of the room.
His prostate had continued growing the entire time he was taking that medication. The 67-year-old, six years on Flomax, kept telling me, "I've got it under control, doctor." He had a pad in his underwear for the leakage the whole time, right up until the maximum dose stopped working altogether.
Ultrasound showed a severely enlarged prostate. Surgery was the only thing left to talk about.
The 71-year-old's urologist literally laughed at him when he said he wanted to try something natural. He was still getting up four to five times a night despite the maximum dose of Tamsulosin. His urinary flow was dreadful.
I see these men at follow-up appointments. They are grateful, and they do not complain.
But I know what they've lost.
They had years to turn this around, to deal with what was really happening inside their prostate, and to protect themselves from an operation they later wished they'd never had. Their bodies had been warning them the whole time. Tamsulosin merely turned the volume down while the real problem carried on growing underneath.
When my own prostate symptoms started at 54, I knew something patients don't. I was waking three, four times a night, with that stuttering stream and the constant feeling of never properly emptying my bladder.
My colleague, Sam Okafor, looked at my symptoms and said what urologists say to every patient. "Standard approach: start with Tamsulosin 0.4 mg, and that'll help the flow." I knew it would help, and I never filled the prescription. As a urologist, I began looking into what was actually happening inside my prostate.
Because I know what an enlarged prostate really is, I knew Tamsulosin was not going to solve it. I spent months going through the research, one journal after another, study after study. What I found made me furious, because I should have known this decades earlier.
This is what urologists don't tell you, because we were never properly trained in it ourselves.
Your prostate does not grow for no reason. When you strip away the noise, it comes down to one thing doing two kinds of damage: DHT.
From your forties onward, your body starts converting testosterone into DHT faster and faster. And DHT is the single thing that drives prostate tissue to keep growing, year after year, without stopping.
That's the first half of it. Your body keeps makin

If your PSA came back high, your biopsy came back clear, and your doctor said “we’ll keep an eye on it,” read this before your next recheck.
My name is Dr. Greg Ellison. I’ve been a urologist for 17 years. I’m about to tell you something that the $12 billion prostate health industry does not want you to hear.
Something that’s going to make every urologist, doctor, and supplement company in America very, very angry.
I’ve been told to stay in my lane. Told that I’m “just a urologist” and I should leave the science to the professionals.
But I don’t care anymore.
Not after watching men go through this twice.
Because a PSA scare the first time is terrifying. A PSA scare the second time is something else entirely.
I see it more than you’d think. A man came in 18 months ago. PSA was 5.8. We ran the tests. Biopsy came back clear. BPH. Enlarged prostate, not cancer. He shook my hand, called his wife, went home relieved.
I told him to come back in 6 months for a recheck.
He came back. PSA was 7.2.
Higher than last time.
And the look on his face was completely different from the first visit.
The first time he was scared. This time he was angry.
Angry at himself for ignoring it for 18 months. Angry at his doctor for saying “we’ll monitor it” and never explaining what they were monitoring for. Angry at the saw palmetto he’d been taking every single day since the first scare, because the number went up anyway. Angry at the system that scared him, relieved him, and then left him sitting with a growing prostate and no answers.
He looked at me and said “I did everything I was supposed to do and the number went up. What am I doing wrong?”
He wasn’t doing anything wrong. That’s the problem.
His prostate was still growing. It was always still growing. The first biopsy told him it wasn’t cancer. It didn’t stop the growth. The saw palmetto on its own didn’t stop the growth. The 6 months of “monitoring” didn’t stop the growth. Nothing he did after that first scare addressed the whole thing that was making his prostate get bigger.
And now the number was higher and the fear was back and this time there was no relief in reserve because he’d used it all up the first time.
I see this pattern constantly. The first scare produces relief. The second scare produces rage. And the rage is pointed in every direction, at the supplement, at the doctor, at himself, except at the thing that’s actually making his life revolve around the bathroom.
I’m telling you now because of what happened in my office two years ago.
A patient named Robert walks in, late 60s, retired engineer.
He’d come in through the same door you just came through. Elevated PSA. Terrified. Tests came back. Not cancer, just BPH. Enlarged prostate.
But his symptoms were bad. He was waking up at 1, 3, and 5 every night, on the dot. He’d stopped drinking water after 6 PM and it didn’t matter. He was mapping the bathrooms at his grandson’s school before games.
He’d already tried the supplements after his cancer scare. Saw palmetto. A bottle of pumpkin seed capsules from the drugstore. Nothing worked. His nights kept getting worse between visits.
I did the workup. Wrote him the script. Either Flomax or Finasteride, the same ones I’ve prescribed thousands of times.
He looked at the paper. Then he looked at me dead in the eyes and said, “I’m not taking this.”
He’d read about the side effects.
The libido issues.
The cognitive fog.
He’d already been through the worst scare of his life. He wasn’t about to trade it for a drug that made him feel like half a man.
He told me, very politely, that he’d rather wake up four times a night than risk what those drugs do to a man’s body.
I tried to talk him through the data. He wasn’t having it. He left without filling the prescription.
Then 3 weeks later, Robert was back in my office for a follow-up.
I expected him to be miserable. Most men in his situation, by week three of refusing treatment, are ready to negotiate.
But I was shocked by what he told me:
“I didn’t ta
